Hi, Doctor. Thanks for fitting me in. I, uh... I hope I'm not wasting your time with this. I guess I came in because things have just been a little off lately. It's hard to explain, but nothing really feels like mine anymore, if that makes sense? 

I mean, I'm a bartender, and I used to actually get a real kick out of making a good drink for a customer. Well, um, I guess my appetite hasn't been great, but I really don't want to make a big deal out of it. I know I probably just need to be more disciplined about my schedule.

Well, um... I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know everybody makes mistakes, and I really try to be a good guy, you know?

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... yeah, I guess it's pretty much every day right now. I really don't want to sound like a broken record, and I know I just talked about this, but... yeah, it's hard to shake. 

Almost every day I wake up and I just keep thinking about how I let down those younger bartenders, and the artists I promised that space to. It just replays in my head. And... I don't know, it just feels like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because I wrecked their shot, you know? 

I really try to push those thoughts away, I do. I try to put on a smile at work and just do my job. I don't want to be a downer for anyone else. But... yeah, it's there most days. Well, um... I really try not to let it affect my job, you know? I've always prided myself on being a hard worker. But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, it has been getting a little harder lately.

I can't seem to make myself go. I tell them I'm just busy or tired because I don't want them to worry or think I'm blowing them off. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent. I'm really trying to keep it together and not let anyone down more than I already have. Is that... does that answer your question? I want to give you the whole picture, even the parts I'm not proud of.

I don't want anyone to think I'm slacking off or anything. Sometimes it just takes me an extra second to get moving, but I'm not, like, moving in slow motion or anything dramatic like that. Well, um... I guess it is pretty bad right now. I don't know if I'd call it the absolute lowest, though. I mean, I'm still trying, right? I'm still getting out of bed most days and trying to go to work. 

I really don't want to sound dramatic or like I'm throwing a pity party. I know a lot of people have it way worse than I do, and I should be grateful I have a job and a place to live. So I suppose I probably have lost some weight because of that. \n\nI'm really trying to force myself to eat more, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm doing this on purpose or just being careless with my health. Is it okay that I don't have an exact number for you? I hope I'm still being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating on tasks or making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But I'm sure it's just from not sleeping well, you know? I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is. \n\nIt's just... well, at work, doing the inventory used to be completely automatic for me. I could do it in my sleep. But lately, I'll be standing there staring at the bottles, and I just... I lose count, like, three times in a row. And honestly... sometimes it feels like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of it. Like I brought it on myself for being such a disappointment to them. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds really dramatic, and I'm really trying not to dwell on it too much. I hope it's okay that I shared that. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Like I said, I just keep thinking about how I let down those younger bartenders, and the local artists I promised wall space to. I just completely failed them. And when I think about how I wrecked their shot at getting noticed... it just makes me feel like I'm completely worthless, you know? Like I'm just taking up space and messing things up for people who actually have potential. \n\nI really try to shake it off, I promise. I know I shouldn't think that way about myself, and I don't want to sound like a burden. It just feels like I deserve whatever bad stuff comes my way because of how much I've disappointed everyone. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Thanks for fitting me in. I, uh... I hope I'm not wasting your time with this. It's hard to explain, but nothing really feels like mine anymore, if that makes sense? \n\nI mean, I'm a bartender, and I used to actually get a real kick out of making a good drink for a customer. Or, you know, going for runs, watching basketball, even just walking around looking at the local murals. I used to get excited about all of that. But lately... I don't know, I just stand there at work, or at home, like I don't care about any of it. It's probably silly to complain about just being bored or whatever, but... is that the sort of thing I should be coming here for?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it's been... maybe about two months now? I hope that's long enough to, you know, justify taking up an appointment. I really don't want to overreact or anything. \n\nBut yeah, it started right around the time the bar got bought out. The new owners came in and kind of... well, they changed everything I had been working toward for years. I know change is just a normal part of the business, and I really shouldn't let it get to me so much. I know I should just roll with it, right? But... yeah, about two months. I don't know, I just kind of go through the motions. I stand there at work, or I sit at home, and I just don't care about any of it anymore. It's like the spark is just... missing. I know I probably sound like I'm just whining about being in a rut, and I really hope I'm not coming across as ungrateful or anything. I just... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is that... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. I, um... I definitely haven't had any thoughts about hurting myself, or... or wishing I wasn't here. \n\nI mean, I know I've been a bit of a downer today, and I'm sorry if I made it sound way worse than it actually is. I just feel kind of blank lately, but I certainly don't want to die. I would never do anything like that. I mean, I know getting older means you slow down a bit, and I'm 51, so I shouldn't expect to have the energy of a kid anymore. I really don't want to sound like I'm just complaining about normal aging, you know? \n\nBut... yeah, it's been harder lately. I'm trying to be accurate for you, but I know it's probably just normal wear and tear from the job.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um, I guess my appetite hasn't been great, but I really don't want to make a big deal out of it. I know I probably just need to be more disciplined about my schedule. \n\nMost days, I just... I kind of forget to eat. Like, I'll be working, and I won't even realize I haven't eaten until my hands start shaking behind the bar. But lately, even just looking at the food kind of makes me feel sick. \n\nI know I should be taking better care of myself, and I'm really sorry if that sounds irresponsible. I'm going to try to do better, I promise. Does that... is that the kind of information you need? I hope I'm not wasting your time with all this.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I've noticed anything like that. I don't want anyone to think I'm slacking off or anything. Sometimes it just takes me an extra second to get moving, but I'm not, like, moving in slow motion or anything dramatic like that. I haven't really noticed any big changes in how I'm moving, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Well, um, I haven't actually weighed myself or anything, so I don't want to give you the wrong information. I know I should probably keep better track of that kind of thing. \n\nBut I guess... yeah, my clothes are definitely fitting a lot looser lately. Like I was saying before about my appetite, I just kind of forget to eat most days until I get those shakes behind the bar. And then even when I do try to force down a few cold bites after closing, I just feel sick looking at it. I don't want you to think I'm doing this on purpose or just being careless with my health. Is it okay that I don't have an exact number for you? I hope I'm still being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating on tasks or making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But I'm sure it's just from not sleeping well, you know? I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is. \n\nIt's just... well, at work, doing the inventory used to be completely automatic for me. I could do it in my sleep. But lately, I'll be standing there staring at the bottles, and I just... I lose count, like, three times in a row. And sometimes I kind of freeze up trying to make really simple decisions, like what garnish I need to restock for the shift. It's so silly, I know. \n\nI'm really trying to focus harder, I promise. I know I just need to pay better attention. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm complaining or making excuses for being bad at my job. Does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know everybody makes mistakes, and I really try to be a good guy, you know? \n\nBut... yeah, I guess I've been feeling pretty bad about some things. It's just... I keep thinking about the younger bartenders at the place, and these local artists I told could hang their stuff up on the walls. I made them these promises, and I just... I feel like I completely let them down. \n\nI don't know, it just gets in my head that I wrecked their shot at getting noticed or getting ahead. And honestly... sometimes it feels like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of it. Like I brought it on myself for being such a disappointment to them. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds really dramatic, and I'm really trying not to dwell on it too much. I hope it's okay that I shared that. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Like I said, I just keep thinking about how I let down those younger bartenders, and the local artists I promised wall space to. I just completely failed them. And when I think about how I wrecked their shot at getting noticed... it just makes me feel like I'm completely worthless, you know? Like I'm just taking up space and messing things up for people who actually have potential. \n\nI really try to shake it off, I promise. I know I shouldn't think that way about myself, and I don't want to sound like a burden. It just feels like I deserve whatever bad stuff comes my way because of how much I've disappointed everyone. Is it normal to feel that way? I hope I'm not making things too difficult for you by saying all this.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think I feel hopeless. I really try to keep my chin up, you know? I don't want to be the kind of guy who just gives up on everything. \n\nI still think... or at least I really hope... that things will work out eventually. I mean, I still want to fix things at the bar and make it up to everyone I disappointed. So, no, I'm definitely not giving up on the future. I'm really trying to stay positive about what's ahead. \n\nI hope it's okay that I say that. I know I've been complaining a lot today, and I really don't want you to think I'm just a total pessimist who sits around waiting for the sky to fall. I want to get better. Am I giving you the right information here? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of worthlessness and guilt nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yeah, I guess it's pretty much every day right now. I really don't want to sound like a broken record, and I know I just talked about this, but... yeah, it's hard to shake. \n\nAlmost every day I wake up and I just keep thinking about how I let down those younger bartenders, and the artists I promised that space to. It just replays in my head. And... I don't know, it just feels like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because I wrecked their shot, you know? \n\nI really try to push those thoughts away, I do. I try to put on a smile at work and just do my job. I don't want to be a downer for anyone else. But... yeah, it's there most days. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much... is this the kind of detail you need? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of worthlessness and guilt are interfering with your ability to function at work or in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to let it affect my job, you know? I've always prided myself on being a hard worker. But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, it has been getting a little harder lately. \n\nI, um... I've missed a few shifts recently, which really isn't like me at all. And I've made some silly mistakes when closing up the bar... things I never used to mess up. Now the other guys have to double-check my work, and it just makes me feel awful because they used to trust me to handle it all perfectly. I'm really trying to get back on track with that, I promise. \n\nAnd outside of work... I guess I haven't been doing much. I used to go running with friends, play some pickup basketball, or go take photos of murals around the city... but I just stopped showing up. I can't seem to make myself go. I tell them I'm just busy or tired because I don't want them to worry or think I'm blowing them off. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent. I'm really trying to keep it together and not let anyone down more than I already have. Is that... does that answer your question? I want to give you the whole picture, even the parts I'm not proud of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of worthlessness and guilt for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess they do kind of stick around for a good chunk of the day. But I really try not to let them take over, you know? \n\nLike, I'll have those thoughts in the morning, but then I try to keep myself busy. I don't want to just sit around wallowing all day—I know that's not productive. So I try to push them to the back of my mind when I'm doing things around the apartment or when I'm at work. Sometimes they creep back in, but I really do my best to shake them off and put on a brave face. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm just sitting there letting it consume me every single minute. I'm fighting it, I really am. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right so you can understand what I'm dealing with.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're at the lowest point you've ever been in your life right now?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it is pretty bad right now. I don't know if I'd call it the absolute lowest, though. I mean, I'm still trying, right? I'm still getting out of bed most days and trying to go to work. \n\nI really don't want to sound dramatic or like I'm throwing a pity party. I know a lot of people have it way worse than I do, and I should be grateful I have a job and a place to live. It's just... it's been a really tough stretch lately, and it feels heavier than it used to. But I'm still fighting it. I really want to get better. \n\nI hope that doesn't make it sound like I'm not taking this seriously, because I am. I just... I want to be honest, but I don't want to sound like I've completely given up, because I haven't. Is that... does that answer what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of worthlessness and guilt are overwhelming to you most of the time?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess they can be, sometimes. I mean, I try not to let them overwhelm me, but... it's hard. \n\nI just keep thinking about the younger guys at the bar, you know?
